NOTICE 
NOTICE is hereby given that the 18th Annual General Meeting of the Members of EMAMI REAL TY LIMITED will 
be held on Tuesday, 29th September, 2026, at 11.30 A.M. through Video Conferencing ("VC") I Other Audio Visual 
Means ("OAVM") to transact the following businesses: 
ORDINARY BUSINESSES: 
1. 
To receive, consider and adopt the Audited Standalone Financial Statements and the Audited 
Consolidated Financial Statements of the Company for the financial year ended 31st March, 2026 together 
with the Reports of the Board of Directors and the Auditors thereon. 
2. 
To appoint a Director in place of Mr. Rajesh Bansal (DIN: 00645035), who retires by rotation and being 
eligible, offers himself for re-appointment. 
SPECIAL BUSINESSES: 
3. 
To consider and, if thought fit, to pass the following Resolution as a Special Resolution:

if any, of the Companies Act, 2013 ("the Act") and the Companies (Appointment and Qualification of 
Directors) Rules, 2014, the applicable provisions of the SEBI (Listing Obligations and Disclosure

2026 and who holds office upto the date of the Annual General Meeting and in respect of whom the 
Company has received a notice in writing under Section 160(1) of the Act from a Member proposing his 
candidature for the office of Director, be and is hereby appointed as a Non-Executive, Non-Independent 
Director of the Company, liable to retire by rotation. 
RESOLVED FURTHER THAT consent of the members of the Company be and is hereby accorded for 
continuation of office of Mr. Ram Krishna Agarwal as a Non-Executive, Non-Independent Director of the 
Company, on his attaining the age of 75 years on 28th August, 2027.

Notes: 
1. 
The Ministry of Corporate Affairs, vide General Circular No. 03/2025 dated 22nd September, 2025 read together 
with other circulars, permitted the holding of the Annual General Meeting ("AGM") through Video Conferencing 
("VC") or Other Audio Visual Means ("OAVM"), without the physical presence of the Members at a common venue. 
In compliance with the provisions of the Companies Act, 2013 (the "Companies Act" or "the Act") and the Securities 
and Exchange Board of India (Listing Obligations and Disclosure Requirements) Regulations 2015 ("SEBI Listing 
Regulations") read with circulars issued by MCA and Securities and Exchange Board of India ("SEBI") from time to 
time, the 18th AGM of the Company is being held through VC/ OAVM. Central Depository Securities Limited 
("CDSL") will be providing facility of remote e-Voting, facility of participation in the AGM through VC/ OAVM and e-
Voting during the AGM. 
2. 
The attendance of the Members attending the AGM through VC/ OAVM will be counted for the purpose of 
ascertaining the quorum under Section 103 of the Companies Act, 2013. 
3. 
As the AGM shall be conducted through VC/ OAVM, the facility for appointment of Proxy by the Members is not 
available for this AGM and hence the Proxy Form and Attendance Slip including Route Map are not annexed to this 
Notice. 
4. 
The Company will conduct the AGM through VC/ OAVM from its Registered Office, i.e, Acropolis, 13th Floor, 1858/1, 
Rajdanga Main Road, Kasba, Kolkata - 700107, which shall be deemed to be the venue of the meeting. 
5. 
The related Explanatory Statement pursuant to Section 102(1) of the Act concerning the business under item No. 
3 to 9 of the Notice is annexed hereto. 
6. 
In accordance with the aforesaid MCA Circulars and SEBI Circulars, the financial statements including report of 
Board of Directors, Auditor's report or other documents required to be attached therewith and the Notice of AGM 
are being sent in electronic mode to Members whose e-mail address is registered with the Company or the 
Depositories/ Depository Participants. Further, in terms of Regulation 36(1)(b) of the SEBI Listing Regulations, a 
letter providing the web-link, including the exact path, where complete details of the Annual Report are available, 
will be sent to those Member(s) whose e-mail address is not registered with the Company or Depository 
Participants. In case any Member is desirous of obtaining hard copy of the Annual Report for the financial year 
2025-26, he/she may send request to the Company's email id at infra@emamirealty.com mentioning folio no., DP 
ID and Client ID.

Procedure for Remote E-Voting and E-Voting during the AGM: 
12. Members may cast their votes on electronic voting system from any place (remote e-voting). The remote e-voting 
period commences on Thursday, 24th September, 2026 (10:00 a.m. IST) and ends on Monday, 28th September, 
2026 (5:00 p.m. IST). During this period, Members holding shares either in physical form or in dematerialized form, 
as on Tuesday, 22nd September, 2026 i.e. cut-off date, may cast their vote electronically. The e-voting module 
shall be disabled by CDSL for voting thereafter. The voting right of shareholders shall be in proportion to their share 
in the paid-up equity share capital of the Company as on the cut-off date. 
13. The Board of Directors has appointed Mr. Manoj Kumar Santhia, Practicing Company Secretary, failing him, Mr. 
Raj Kumar Santhia, Practicing Company Secretary, both being Partners of M/s. MKS & Associates, Company 
Secretaries, Kolkata to act as the Scrutinizer to scrutinize the remote e-voting process in a fair and transparent 
manner. 
14. The procedure fore-Voting on the day of the AGM is same as the instructions mentioned below for Remote e-
voting. 
15. Only those shareholders, who are present in the AGM through VC/OAVM facility and have not casted their vote on 
the Resolutions through remote e-Voting and are otherwise not barred from doing so, shall be eligible to vote 
through e-Voting system available during the AGM. 
16. If any vote is cast by the shareholders through the e-voting facility available during the AGM and if the same 
shareholders have not participated in the meeting through VC/ OAVM facility, then the votes cast by such 
shareholders shall be considered invalid as the facility of e-voting during the meeting is available only to the 
shareholders attending the meeting. 
17. The Members who have cast their vote by remote e-voting prior to the AGM may also attend/ participate in the AGM 
through VC I OAVM but shall not be entitled to cast their vote again. 
18. The voting rights of Members shall be in proportion to their shares in the paid-up equity share capital of the Company 
as on the cut-off date. A person who is not a Member as on the cut-off date should treat this Notice of AGM for 
information purpose only.

## Page 9

Step 2 
Access through CDSL e-Voting system in case of shareholders holding shares in physical mode and 
non-individual shareholders in demat mode. 
(i) The voting period begins on Thursday, 24th September, 2026 (10:00 a.m. IST) and ends on Monday, 28th 
September, 2026 (5:00 p.m. IST). During this period shareholders' of the Company, holding shares either in 
physical form or in dematerialized form, as on the cut-off date (record date) of Tuesday, 22nd September, 2026 
may cast their vote electronically. Thee-voting module shall be disabled by CDSL for voting thereafter. 
(ii) Shareholders who have already voted prior to the meeting date would not be entitled to vote at the meeting 
venue. 
(iii) Pursuant to SEBI Circular No. SEBl/HO/CFD/CMD/CIR/P/2020/242 dated 09.12.2020, under Regulation 44 of 
Securities and Exchange Board of India (Listing Obligations and Disclosure Requirements) Regulations, 2015, 
listed entities are required to provide remote e-voting facility to its shareholders, in respect of all shareholders' 
resolutions. However, it has been observed that the participation by the public non-institutional shareholders/ 
retail shareholders is at a negligible level. 
Currently, there are multiple e-voting service providers (ESPs) providing e-voting facility to listed entities in 
India. This necessitates registration on various ESPs and maintenance of multiple user IDs and passwords by 
the shareholders. 
In order to increase the efficiency of the voting process, pursuant to a public consultation, it has been decided 
to enable e-voting to all the demat account holders, by way of a single login credential, through their 
demat accounts/ websites of Depositories/ Depository Participants. Demat account holders would be 
able to cast their vote without having to register again with the ESPs, thereby, not only facilitating seamless 
authentication but also enhancing ease and convenience of participating in e-voting process. 
Step 1 
Access through Depositories CDSL/NSDL e-Voting system in case of individual shareholders holding 
shares in demat mode. 
(iv) In terms of SEBI circular no. SEBl/HO/CFD/CMD/CIR/P/2020/242 dated December 9, 2020 on e-Voting 
facility provided by Listed Companies, Individual shareholders holding securities in demat mode are allowed 
to vote through their demat account maintained with Depositories and Depository Participants. Shareholders 
are advised to update their mobile number and email Id in their demat accounts in order to access e-Voting 
facility.

22. Instructions for shareholders attending the AGM through VC/OAVM & e-voting during Meeting are as under: 
a. 
The procedure for attending meeting & e-Voting on the day of the AGM is same as the instructions mentioned 
above fore-voting. 
b. 
The link for VC/OAVM to attend meeting will be available where the EVSN of Company will be displayed after 
successful login as per the instructions mentioned above fore-voting. 
c. 
Shareholders who have voted through Remote e-Voting will be eligible to attend the meeting. However, they 
will not be eligible to vote at the AGM. 
d. 
Shareholders are encouraged to join the Meeting through Laptops I I Pads for better experience. 
e. 
Further shareholders will be required to allow Camera and use Internet with a good speed to avoid any 
disturbance during the meeting. 
f. 
Please note that Participants Connecting from Mobile Devices or Tablets or through Laptop connecting via 
Mobile Hotspot may experience Audio/Video loss due to Fluctuation in their respective network. It is therefore 
recommended to use Stable Wi-Fi or LAN Connection to mitigate any kind of aforesaid glitches. 
g. 
Only those shareholders, who are present in the AGM through VC/OAVM facility and have not casted their 
vote on the Resolutions through remote e-Voting and are otherwise not barred from doing so, shall be eligible 
to vote through e-Voting system available during the AGM. 
h. 
If any Votes are cast by the shareholders through the e-voting available during the AGM and if the same 
shareholders have not participated in the meeting through VC/OAVM facility, then the votes cast by such 
shareholders may be considered invalid as the facility of e-voting during the meeting is available only to the 
shareholders attending the meeting.

do not wish to speak during the AGM but have queries may send their queries in advance 1 O (TEN) days prior 
to meeting mentioning their name, demat account number/folio number, email id, mobile number at 
infra@ernarnirealty.com. These queries will be replied to by the Company suitably. 
b. Those shareholders who have registered themselves as a speaker will only be allowed to express their 
views/ask questions during the meeting.

by the Board. 
26. The results of the e-Voting shall be declared to the Stock Exchanges by Thursday, October 01, 2026. The results 
along with the consolidated Scrutinizer's Report shall be placed on the Company's website www.emamirealty.com 
and on the website of CDSL www.evotingindia.com immediately after the results. Subject to receipt of requisite 
number of votes, the resolutions set out in the Notice shall be deemed to be passed on the date of the AGM i.e. 
September 29, 2026.

30. Members may please note that SEBI vide its Circular No. SEBl/HO/MIRSD/MIRSD_ RTAMB/P/CIR/2022/8 dated 
January 25, 2022 has mandated the listed companies to issue securities in dematerialized form only while processing 
service requests viz. Issue of duplicate securities certificate; claim from unclaimed suspense account; renewal I 
exchange of securities certificate; endorsement; subdivision I splitting of securities certificate; consolidation of 
securities certificates I folios; transmission and transposition. Accordingly, Members are requested to make service 
requests by submitting a duly filled and signed Form ISR - 4, the format of which is available on the Company's 
website at https://www.emamirealty.com/investors/. It may be noted that any service request can be processed only 
after the folio is KYC compliant. SEBI has mandated that all requests for transfer of securities including transmission 
and transposition requests shall be processed only in dematerialised form. In view of the same and to eliminate all 
risks associated with physical shares and avail various benefits of dematerialisation, Members are advised to 
dematerialise the shares held by them in physical form. Members can contact the Company or the RTA for 
assistance in this regard.

---


## Page 14

In furtherance of the Government of India's initiatives on "Ease of Doing Business" and "Ease of Doing Investment", 
SEBI has dispensed with the requirement of issuance of Letter of Confirmation ("LOC") for effecting direct credit of 
securities to the dematerialised account of investors. This change aims to speed up the process significantly and 
has become effective from April 02, 2026. LOCs issued earlier remain valid for dematerialisation within 120 days. 
Under the new framework, investors must already hold a demat account and submit a duly attested, recent Client 
Master List along with prescribed forms. RTA, Companies and Depositories are required to complete verification 
and credit securities directly into the demat account within 30 days of the receipt of such request. Accordingly, 
Members are requested to make service requests to the Company through e-mail: infra@emamirealty.com or by 
writing to the Company's RTA at contact@mdplcorporate.com by submitting a duly filled and signed Form ISR-4 & 
ISR-5, as the case may be along with recent Client Master List. 
31. SEBI vide Circular no. SEBl/HO/OIAE/OIAE_IAD-1/P/ CIR/2023/131dated31July,2023 as amended from time to 
time, has specified that a shareholder shall first take up any grievance with the listed entity by lodging a complaint 
directly with the Company/RTA by sending an email to infra@emamirealty.com or contact@mdplcorporate.com and 
if the grievance is not redressed satisfactorily, the shareholder may, in accordance with the SCORES guidelines, 
escalate the same through the SCORES Portal (https://scores.sebi.gov.in} in accordance with the process laid out 
therein. Only after exhausting all available options for resolution of the grievance, if the shareholder is not satisfied 
with the outcome, he/she/they can initiate dispute resolution through the Online Dispute Resolution ("ODR") Portal 
at https://smartodr.in/login. Shareholders are requested to take note of the same. The aforesaid SEBI Circular can 
be viewed on the following link https://www.sebi.gov.in/legal/master-circulars/dec-2023/master-circular-for-online-
resolution-of-disputes-in-the-indian-securities-market 80236.html. 
32. SEBI vide Circular no. H0/38/13/11 (2) 2026-MIRSD-POD/1/3750/2026 dated January 30, 2026, in order to facilitate 
the investors to get rightful access to their securities, has decided to open special window for re-lodgement of transfer 
deeds of physical securities i.e, transfer and dematerialisation of physical securities which were sold or purchased 
prior to April 1, 2019. The special window is open for a period of one year from February 5, 2026 to February 4, 
2027. The special window shall also be available for such transfer requests which were submitted earlier and were 
rejected/returned/not attended to due to deficiency in the documents/ process/or otherwise.

EXPLANATORY STATEMENT PURSUANT TO SECTION 102 OF THE COMPANIES ACT, 2013 
ITEM NO. 3 
The Nomination and Remuneration Committee of the Company, taking into consideration the skills, expertise and 
competencies required for the Board in the context of the business and sectors of the Company, at their meeting 
held on 27th May, 2026 has recommended appointment of Mr. Ram Krishna Agarwal as Non-Executive Non-
independent Director of the Company. Based on the same, the Board of Directors, at its Meeting held on 27th May 
2026 has appointed Mr. Ram Krishna Agarwal as an as an Additional Director in the category Non-Executive Non-
independent Director of the Company, with effect from 1st July, 2026. In terms of Section 161(1) of the Act and the 
Articles of Association of the Company, Mr. Agarwal holds office upto the date of this Annual General Meeting 
('AGM') and is eligible for appointment as a Director. The Company has received notice under Section 160 of the 
Act from a shareholder proposing the candidature of Mr. Agarwal for the office of Non-Executive Director of the 
Company. 
Mr. Ram Krishna Agarwal is a qualified Chartered Accountant and has the distinction of securing First Rank on an 
All-India basis in both the Intermediate and Final Examinations of the Institute of Chartered Accountants of India 
and being awarded Gold Medals for the same. He was associated with S. R. Batliboi & Co. (Member Firm of EY) 
as a Partner since 1978 and was the Managing Partner of the firm at the time of his retirement in June 2013. He 
was also a Director of Ernst & Young India Pvt. Ltd. and served as National Head -Assurance Services of Ernst 
& Young in India. He has over 50 years of post-qualification experience in audit, taxation, company law, 
consultancy, mergers and acquisitions, due diligence, valuation and related areas, with extensive exposure to 
diverse industries including engineering, construction and real estate, both in India and abroad. He has also held 
various positions in professional and industry bodies including the Institute of Chartered Accountants of India, The 
Institute of Internal Auditors and the Confederation of Indian Industry. 
Mr. Ram Krishna Agarwal has given his consent in writing to act as a Director and an intimation to the effect that 
he is not disqualified under Section 164(2) of the Companies Act, 2013. Mr. Agarwal is not debarred from holding 
the office of director by virtue of any SEBI order or any other such authority. 
The Board is of the opinion that Mr. Agarwal possesses the identified core skills, expertise and competencies 
fundamental for effective functioning in his role as a Director of the Company and considers that the association 
of Mr. Agarwal would be of immense benefit to the Company. Mr. Agarwal will be attaining the age of 75 years on 
28th August, 2027. This approval of shareholders by special resolution must also be taken as approval under 
Regulation 17(1A) of the SEBI (Listing Obligations and Disclosure Requirements) Regulations, 2015 ("SEBI Listing 
Regulations") for his continuance as non-executive director after attaining the age of 75 years. 
The Board, therefore, recommends the special resolution for appointment of Mr. Ram Krishna Agarwal as Non-
Executive Non-Independent Director of the Company for approval of the members. The Special Resolution at Item 
No. 3 seeks approval and consent of the members for appointment of Mr. R K Agarwal as Non-Executive Non-
independent Director of the Company with effect from 1st July, 2026 and continuing in office as such, on his 
attaining the age of 75 years. Mr. Agarwal shall be paid remuneration by way of sitting fees for attending meetings 
of the Board or Committees thereof, as applicable, and reimbursement of expenses for participating in such 
meetings.

ITEM NO. 4 AND 5 
The Members of the Company had, at the Annual General Meeting held on 21st September, 2017, approved a 
Special Resolution pursuant to Section 180(1 )(c) of the Companies Act, 2013 ("the Act"), authorising the Board of 
Directors of the Company to borrow monies in excess of the aggregate of the paid-up share capital, free reserves 
and securities premium of the Company. Further, the Members of the Company had, on 27th May, 2020, approved 
a Special Resolution pursuant to Section 180(1 )(a) of the Act, authorising the Board to create mortgages, charges 
and hypothecation over the properties and assets of the Company for securing borrowings and other financial 
obligations. 
Considering the present and future business requirements of the Company, including requirements for working 
capital, project funding, capital expenditure, general corporate purposes and other business requirements, it is 
considered appropriate to renew and reaffirm the borrowing powers of the Board and to supersede the earlier 
resolution relating to creation of security and provide an overall limit of~ 5,000 Crores (Rupees Five Thousand 
Crores only). 
Accordingly, the proposed resolutions seek approval of the Members to authorise the Board to borrow, from time 
to time, any sum or sums of money, whether by way of loans, debentures, bonds, other debt securities or otherwise, 
whether secured or unsecured, from banks, financial institutions, bodies corporate or any other 
person(s)/entity(ies), notwithstanding that the monies so borrowed together with the monies already borrowed by 
the Company, apart from temporary loans obtained or to be obtained from the Company's bankers in the ordinary 
course of business, may exceed the aggregate of the paid-up share capital, free reserves and securities premium 
of the Company, provided that the total outstanding borrowings of the Company shall not, at any time, exceed ~ 
5,000 Crores (Rupees Five Thousand Crores only). 
The proposed resolutions further seek to authorise the Board to create mortgages, charges and hypothecation, in 
addition to the existing mortgages, charges and hypothecation created by the Company, on all or any of the 
movable and immovable properties of the Company, wherever situated, both present and future, and the whole or 
any part of the undertaking of the Company, in favour of financial institutions, banks, lenders, investing agencies 
or any other person(s)/bodies corporate, for securing rupee/foreign currency loans and/or issue of debentures, 
bonds or other financial instruments availed by the Company and/or or any third party, including the related parties. 
The Board recommends the Special Resolutions set out at Item No. 4 and 5 of the Notice for approval of the 
Members. 
None of the Directors, Key Managerial Personnel of the Company or their respective relatives are concerned or 
interested, financially or otherwise, in the said Resolutions, except to the extent of their respective shareholding, if 
any, in the Company. 
ITEM NO. 6 
Upon recommendation of the Audit Committee, the Board of Directors in its meeting held on 13th August, 2026, 
has re-appointed M/s. V. K. Jain & Co., Cost Accountants (Firm Registration No 00049), as Cost Auditors of the 
Company, to conduct the audit of the cost records of the Company for the financial year 2026-27 at a remuneration

---


## Page 17

of ~30,000/- (Rupees Thirty Thousand only), exclusive of applicable taxes and out of pocket expenses. 
Considering the scope of audit, time and resources deployed by the cost auditors, in the opinion of the Board of 
Directors, the proposed remuneration payable would be fair and reasonable and do not in any way impair the 
independence and judgment of the Cost Auditors. 
As per the provisions of Section 148 of the Companies Act, 2013 and the Rules made thereunder, the remuneration 
to be paid to the cost auditors is subject to ratification by the members of the Company. Accordingly, your Directors 
seek your approval to the resolution at item no. 6 by way of an ordinary resolution, for ratification of payment of 
remuneration to the Cost Auditors for Financial years 2026-27. 
None of the Directors or Key Managerial Personnel of the Company and their relatives, are concerned or interested, 
financially or otherwise in this Resolution. 
ITEM NO. 7 
The Members of the Company, at the 17th Annual General Meeting held on 24th September, 2025, approved the 
re-appointment of Dr. Nitesh Kumar Gupta {DIN: 08756907) as the Managing Director, designated as Managing 
Director & CEO of the Company, for a period of five years with effect from 30th June, 2025, together with 
remuneration comprising Basic Salary in the range of ~7,50,000/- to ~15,00,000/- per month, along with applicable 
perquisites, allowances and other benefits. The Nomination and Remuneration Committee, at its meeting held on 
27th May, 2026, considered and recommended the revision in the remuneration structure of Dr. Nitesh Kumar 
Gupta. 
Pursuant to the applicable provisions of the Labour Codes and the consequential changes in the definition of 
"wages", the Company has revised the remuneration structure of its employees, including the Managing Director, 
with effect from 1st April, 2026. Under the revised remuneration structure, the Basic' Salary component has been 
revised from 40% of CTC to 50% of CTC, with a corresponding adjustment in other components of remuneration, 
while keeping the overall CTC unchanged. Consequent to the aforesaid restructuring, the Basic Salary payable to 
Dr. Nitesh Kumar Gupta exceeded the maximum Basic Salary of ~15,00,000/- per month approved by the 
Members, without any increase in the overall CTC and the revision made with corresponding adjustment in other 
components of remuneration. Accordingly, it is proposed to ratify the payment of increased basic salary and to 
enhance the approved Basic Salary range from V,50,000/- to ~15,00,000/- per month to ~15,00,000/- to 
~35,00,000/- per month, with effect from 1st April, 2026. 
The proposed revision is primarily intended to give effect to the revised remuneration structure consequent upon 
the implementation of the Labour Codes and to ratify the Basic Salary paid/payable to Dr. Nitesh Kumar Gupta 
with effect from 1s1 April, 2026. The revised range will also provide adequate headroom for future increments, as 
may be recommended by the Nomination and Remuneration Committee and approved by the Board, based on his 
performance evaluation and in accordance with the applicable provisions of law. 
The Board of Directors, at its meeting held on 27th May, 2026, after considering the recommendation of the 
Nomination and Remuneration Committee, approved the aforesaid revision in remuneration, subject to the 
approval of the Members of the Company. 
The Company proposes to enter into a Supplemental Agreement with Dr. Nitesh Kumar Gupta incorporating the 
aforesaid revision and consequential amendments to the Managing Director & CEO's Agreement dated 22°d May, 
2025. Except for the aforesaid revision in remuneration, all other terms and conditions of the appointment and 
remuneration of Dr. Nitesh Kumar Gupta as Managing Director & CEO shall remain unchanged. The particulars 
as required to be disclosed pursuant to the provisions of Section 102 of the Companies Act, 2013, read with the

---


## Page 18

applicable provisions of the Companies (Appointment and Remuneration of Managerial Personnel) Rules, 2014 
and Schedule V to the Act, has been provided in the Annexure 2 to the Notice. 
The Board recommends the resolution set out at Item No. 7 of the accompanying Notice for approval of the 
Members by way of Special Resolution. 
Except Dr. Nitesh Kumar Gupta and his relatives, none of the Directors, Key Managerial Personnel of the Company 
and their relatives is concerned or interested, financially or otherwise, in the resolution.

inspection by the Members at the registered office of the Company during business hours on any working day, up 
to the date of the meeting and shall also be made available for inspection at the AGM in accordance with applicable 
law. 
ITEM NO. 8 
The Company had entered into a Related Party Transaction with Orbit Projects Private Limited ("Orbit Projects"), 
a related party of the Company, during the financial year 2025-26, in relation to the slump sale/transfer of the 
Project Undertaking of the Company, together with all assets, liabilities, rights, obligations, licences, permits, 
contracts and other incidental and ancillary rights forming part of such undertaking, for an aggregate consideration 
of ~28.50 Crores (Rupees Twenty-Eight Crores and Fifty Lakhs only). The said transaction was undertaken in the 
ordinary course of business and on an arm's length basis. The Members had previously approved the said 
transaction for an aggregate value of ~27 .00 Crores. However, the actual aggregate value of the transaction 
undertaken during the financial year 2025-26 amounted to ~28.50 Crores, thereby exceeding the previously 
approved limit by ~1.50 Crores. 
Accordingly, approval of the Members is being sought for ratification and approval of the Related Party Transaction 
of ~28.50 Crores, including the excess amount over the limit previously approved by the Members, in accordance 
with the applicable provisions of Section 188 and other applicable provisions of the Companies Act, 2013 ("the 
Act"), read with the Rules made thereunder, Regulations 2(1)(zc) and 23 of the SEBI Listing Regulations, the 
Company's Policy on Related Party Transactions and other applicable laws, rules and regulations. 
The Audit Committee of the Company has reviewed the relevant details and documents pertaining to the 
transaction and has considered the same in accordance with the applicable provisions of the Act, SEBI Listing 
Regulations, the Company's Policy on Related Party Transactions and the applicable Industry Standards on 
"Minimum Information to be provided to the Audit Committee and Shareholders for Approval of Related Party 
Transactions". The Audit Committee has approved/recommended the transaction and its ratification by the 
Members, noting that the transaction was undertaken on an arm's length basis and in the ordinary course of 
business of the Company. 
The Board of Directors, based on the recommendation of the Audit Committee, is of the opinion that the ratification 
and approval of the aforesaid Related Party Transaction is in the best interest of the Company and its shareholders 
and accordingly recommends the Ordinary Resolution set out at Item No. 8 for approval of the Members. 
The mandatory disclosure which is required to be made to the Shareholders in accordance with the Industry 
Standards on "Minimum Information to be provided to the Audit Committee and Shareholders for Approval of 
Related Party Transactions" dated June 26, 2025 read with SEBI Master Circular dated January 30, 2026, is 
annexed to this Notice as Annexure 3.

ITEM NO. 9 
Vijaybhan Investments and Consultancy Private Limited ("Vijaybhan") is a private limited company incorporated 
on 20th Feb 1995 and having its registered office at Alind Kavinpuram, Puliyarakkonam Trivandrum, Neyyattinkara, 
Kerala - 695573, India. 
Vijaybhan had issued Zero Coupon Unsecured Optionally Convertible Non-Transferable Debentures ("OCDs") of 
~1,00,000/- each, subject to the terms and conditions approved by its members at their general meeting held on 
26th April, 2016. Pursuant to the scheme of merger of Zandu Realty Limited with the Company, sanctioned by the 
Hon'ble National Company Law Tribunal, Kolkata Bench, vide its Order dated 4th May, 2018 (with effect from 1st 
April, 2017), 6,716 OCDs of face value ~1,00,000/- each, aggregating to ~67, 16,00,000/-, vested in the Company. 
Subsequently, Care Integrated Private Limited was merged with Vijaybhan with effect from 7th March, 2018. 
Pursuant to this merger, the promoters and promoter group of the Company, who were shareholders of Care 
Integrated Private Limited, were allotted shares in Vijaybhan and consequently came to hold about 49.59% of the 
equity share capital of Vijaybhan. As a result, Vijaybhan became a related party of the Company within the meaning 
of Regulation 2(1 )(zb) of the Listing Regulations. 
The Finance Committee of the Board of Directors of the Company have approved further investments by the 
Company in such OCDs. As on 31st December, 2025, the Company held 10,545 {Ten Thousand Five Hundred and 
Forty-five) Nos of OCDs of face value of Rs. 1,00,000/- each, aggregating to ~ 105.45 Crores. 
The Finance Committee, at its meeting held on 5th January, 2026, approved the modification of the terms of the 
said 10,545 OCDs to provide for their conversion into Redeemable Preference Shares, on such terms and 
conditions as may be mutually agreed. Accordingly, pursuant to Sections 55, 71 and other applicable provisions 
of the Companies Act, 2013, the said OCDs were converted into Class B Redeemable Preference Shares ("Class 
B RPS") of face value ~1,00,000/- each, in the ratio of 1 OCD : 1 Class B RPS, on the terms set out below:

Redemption Premium

The exposure to Vijaybhan is an ongoing one that vested in the Company pursuant to the merger of Zandu Realty 
Limited with the Company, as sanctioned by the Hon'ble NCL T, Kolkata Bench, at a time when Vijaybhan was not 
a related party of the Company. Vijaybhan became a related party only subsequently, upon the merger of Care 
Integrated Private Limited with Vijaybhan, pursuant to which the promoters of the Company acquired 49.59% of 
the equity share capital of Vijaybhan. The investment was, therefore, not made as a fresh related party transaction 
entered into at the Company's discretion in the ordinary sense, but arose as a consequence of the said court-
sanctioned merger, with the related party relationship arising only at a later stage, and subsequent incremental 
investments approved by the Finance Committee of the Board in the ordinary course. The conversion of the OCDs 
into Class B RPS was undertaken to provide for a more structured and defined return profile on the said investment. 
The aforesaid investments in OCDs of Vijaybhan and their subsequent conversion into Class B RPS, though 
approved by the Finance Committee of the Board from time to time, were inadvertently not placed before the Audit 
Committee and the shareholders of the Company for prior approval as required under Regulation 23 of the SEBI 
Listing Regulations. This omission was on account of an inadvertent oversight and not on account of any mala fide 
intent. 
The Audit Committee has, at its meeting held on 23rd February, 2026, on the basis of a thorough scrutiny of relevant 
details I documents provided by the Management and also of the mandatory disclosure which is required to be 
made to the Audit Committee in accordance with the Industry Standards on "Minimum Information to be provided 
to the Audit Committee and Shareholders for Approval of Related Party Transactions" dated June 26, 2025 read 
with SEBI Master Circular dated January 30, 2026, reviewed the said transactions and accorded its ratification and 
approval thereto, and recommended that the same be placed before the shareholders of the Company for 
ratification. The Company has since strengthened its internal processes to ensure that all related party 
transactions, including those approved by Committees of the Board, are placed before the Audit Committee and 
shareholders (as applicable) in a timely manner, in compliance with Section 188 of the Act and Regulation 23 of 
the SEBI Listing Regulations. 
As per the certificate received from the Managing Director & CEO and the President - Finance & CFO of the 
Company which was placed before the Audit Committee and the Board of Directors, the transactions is not, in any 
manner, detrimental to the interest of minority shareholders and is in the best interest of the Company and its 
shareholders. 
In terms of Regulation 23 of the SEBI Listing Regulations, the aforesaid transaction constitute a material related 
party transaction, as the same exceeds the threshold prescribed under Regulation 23(1 ). The Board of Directors, 
based on the recommendation of the Audit Committee, is of the view that the aforesaid transaction is in the ordinary

---

course of business of the Company and are on an arm's length basis. Accordingly, approval of the shareholders 
of the Company by way of an Ordinary Resolution is being sought for ratification of the said transaction.

II. Information about the Appointee: 
1. Background details/ Job profile I suitability 
Dr. Nitesh Kumar Gupta is a PhD as well as a Double Post-Graduate in Management. With a trailblazing 
experience of over 33 years, he has served over 18 years as Senior level Management. Dr. Nitesh Kumar plays 
a pivotal role in the Company's projects having pan India presence. His fields of expertise pans through real 
estate development, strategic planning, marketing, mergers & acquisitions and branding. He is a keen planner 
and strategist with expertise in new product launching, new business & market development, product 
positioning and achieving sales and profitability targets for the company. Having qualities of an inspirational 
leader coupled with entrepreneurial skills, strong business acumen and analytical skills, he believes in ground 
zero hands-on management to improvise proven management tools. Leading from the front, Dr. Nitesh Kumar 
evolves a team culture that emphasizes the importance of members and recognizes the value they bring to the 
organization. He has amassed extensive industry experience in identifying growth opportunities and 
undertaking negotiations for strategic partnerships in new markets. His across-the-board experience and 
strategic decision making has facilitated writing several commercial success stories.

---


## Page 24

2. Past remuneration: 
Dr. Nitesh Kumar received remuneration of~ 285.66 Lakhs in FY 2025-26 and ~ 193.99 Lakhs in FY 2024-25. 
3. Recognition or awards: 
Dr. Nitesh Kumar Gupta has consistently distinguished himself as a visionary and transformative leader in the 
real estate and construction industry. Over the years, his exceptional leadership and strategic foresight have 
earned him numerous accolades across prestigious industry platforms. 
In 2025, Dr. Nitesh Kumar Gupta was honored with the "Iconic Leader of the Year" award at the 17th Realty+ 
Conclave & Excellence Awards (East). The previous year, he received the "CXO of the Year (Individual 
Assessment Award)" at the 16th edition of the same awards. His dedication to sustainability and innovation 
was recognized in 2023, when he was named "Sustainable Business Leader of the Year" at the 15th Realty+ 
Conclave & Excellence Awards (East). In 2022, he was awarded the "Realty Personality of the Year" at the 
Real Estate Conclave & Awards (East). 
That same year, Dr. Nitesh Kumar Gupta was also conferred with the "CEO of the Year" title for Excellence & 
Leadership in Real Estate & Construction at the Business Innovation Conference & Awards. His achievements 
in 2021 were especially noteworthy, with multiple honors including "Regional Winner - East" at the CWAB 
Awards, "Inspirational Leader" by the Dare2Dream Awards presented by TV9 Network, and the "Pathfinders 
Award for the Most Enterprising CXO" at the Estate Awards by RE/MAX India. 
Additionally, he was recognized with a Certificate of Excellence by Realty+ 50 Marketing Minds 2021, listed 
among "India's Greatest Leaders 2021-22" by AsiaOne, and honored for his "Outstanding Contribution to the 
Industry" at the 13th Annual Estate Awards 2021. He also received the "CXO of the Year" award at the Realty+ 
Excellence & Conclave Awards 2021 (East) and was named the "Most Enterprising CXO of the Year", further 
solidifying his reputation as an influential and forward-thinking leader. 
4. Remuneration proposed: As set out in the Explanatory Statement

## Page 25

As per applicable accounting standards, revenue and profit is recognised 
as per project completion method i.e. upon receipt of occupancy certificate 
for respective projects. The profit of the Company in a particular financial 
year will largely be dependent on the completion of the project I phase rather 
on sale of units in the respective financial year and other relevant factors. 
Therefore, all projects which are at various stages of completion are not 
contributing to the revenue and profit for particular year, until completion. 
In addition, there are fixed cost and interest expenditure which are expensed 
out in the Profit & Loss Account. Due to the project completion accounting 
system that the Company follows, the profit for the year may be inadequate, 
even though the Company would have recorded good booking value. 
Normal product cycle of a project in real estate company is 4-6 years. Hence 
looking at the response, which the Company's projects have been 
witnessing, the Company is very positive that in the coming years, we will 
be able to show stronger results than before.

Premium ofup to ~4,50,000 per share, together with 
an Additional Premium of 15% per annum 
(computed on the aggregate of face value and 
Redemption Premium) if redeemed after March 31, 
2026; ( d) No voting rights, except as provided under 
Section 47(2) of the Companies Act, 2013; and (e) 
No variation of rights without a separate resolution 
of the Class B RPS holders voting as a separate class.

Corporate Overview
Every enduring story begins with a belief. Emami began with the belief that an idea, 
when guided by courage and conviction, could grow into something much larger than 
itself. What started as a humble beginning gradually became a journey of ambition, 
entrepreneurship and enterprise, shaped by the vision of Shri R. S. Agarwal and 
Shri R. S. Goenka and carried forward by generations of people who believed in the 
possibilities ahead.
Over the years, the world around Emami changed. Markets evolved, aspirations 
expanded and new opportunities emerged. Yet, at every turn, Emami continued to 
adapt without losing its essence, to evolve without forgetting its roots and to grow 
while remaining anchored in trust.
A world of possibilities ahead And 
a legacy that continues to move 
forward.
From a Beginning to a New 
Horizon
For Emami Realty, this legacy is both a foundation and an inspiration. We 
carry forward the values that shaped the past while imagining spaces that 
respond to the aspirations of tomorrow. With every development, the 
endeavour is to create enduring value, enrich lives and remain open to the 
possibilities that lie ahead.
Because the future is never simply built; it is envisioned first.
Enterprise
Evolution
Endless
Through every phase of this journey, one spirit 
has remained constant which is the spirit of 
enterprise, evolution and endless possibility. It is 
this spirit that has enabled Emami to look beyond 
the present, embrace change with confidence 
and continually explore new avenues for growth.

## Page 41

4
Annual Report 2025-26
Emami Realty’s roadmap reflects a deliberate transition from 
immediate asset optimization to sustained regional leadership.
In a shifting macro-economic 
environment, the strategy 
is anchored in a clear and 
progressive roadmap...
... by firstly, stabilising our fiscal foundation by extracting full value 
from existing assets; then, pursuing calculated expansion once that 
foundation is secure.
This is not a pause in ambition, but a disciplined precondition for it. By 
prioritizing internal capital recycling and operational de-risking in the near 
term, we build the resilience required to lead decisively in the years ahead. 
Every step is sequential by design, capital secured today becomes the 
confidence to expand tomorrow.
The result is a self-sustaining cycle: Liquidity and stability are built first, 
generating the foundation that fuels increasingly aggressive growth, 
ensuring expansion is never over-leveraged and dominance, when it 
comes, stands on solid ground.
Emami Aamod Basket Ball Court (Artistic Image)
5
Annual Report 2025-26
Financial Statements
Statutory Reports
Corporate Overview
Internal Asset Monetization 
Our current financial strength is engineered through the disciplined 
monetization of existing land banks. Moving beyond a single holding 
strategy, we operate a three-pronged approach. Direct development for 
end-to-end value creation, as-is sales for immediate liquidity and strategic 
retention for long-term appreciation.
It is a quiet, deliberate phase of internal stabilization, converting balance-
sheet potential into tangible readiness. Every asset monetized today is a 
deliberate step toward the capital base that will fund our next chapter, 
expansion into West Bengal’s rising opportunity landscape.

## Page 42

6
Annual Report 2025-26
Regional 
Expansion: 
West Bengal
From here the focus pivots toward 
external growth, anchored in West 
Bengal, our home ground and our 
conviction. A changed investment 
climate has repositioned the region as 
a preferred destination for domestic 
and global capital, backed by an 
estimated `1 lakh crore in committed 
investment.
Four forces drive this focus: proximity to 
headquarters, an established brand moat in 
Kolkata, expanding infrastructure corridors, 
a surge in IT and Global Capability Centre 
demand. Together, they are reshaping the 
region into a high-yield market for premium 
real estate.
This expansion is not opportunistic, it is the 
natural extension of strength built at home. 
By cementing our base first, we ensure 
our growth into new territory is deliberate, 
informed and sustainable, rather than 
reactive to market cycles.

Corporate Overview
To meet rising demand, our portfolio has evolved. Our 
aim is to achieve 80% residential, with focus on upper-
mid and premium segments and 20% commercial, 
capturing Grade-A office demand from the IT and GCC 
influx.
Delivering at this standard requires more than 
construction, it demands a technology-driven 
operating model. Aluminium formwork, digital 
governance systems, real-time project tracking and AI-
enabled customer support ensure premium delivery is 
repeatable, not incidental.
Portfolio, Technology and Legacy
From advanced construction 
technologies and real-time quality 
monitoring to AI-powered customer 
engagement, Emami Realty is leveraging 
technology to drive greater efficiency, 
transparency and precision across the 
project lifecycle.

Every city tells a story and every skyline reflects the dreams 
of those who shape it. At Emami Realty, we believe that every 
space has the power to inspire, every address can nurture a 
community and every development can leave a lasting imprint 
on generations to come.
Headquartered in Kolkata, Emami Realty Limited is a leading real estate developer with a diversified 
portfolio across residential, commercial and mixed-use developments. Over the years, the Company 
has built a strong reputation for creating thoughtfully designed spaces that combine architectural 
excellence, superior construction quality and modern amenities, while establishing a growing 
presence across India’s key urban markets.
Our customer-first philosophy and a commitment to innovation, continues to unlock value from its strategically located 
land bank, accelerate the execution of ongoing developments and pursue opportunities in high-potential growth

Annual Report 2025-26
It gives us immense pride to address you at a 
moment we consider genuinely pivotal in the 
journey of Emami Realty. The year gone by has 
tested businesses across the country with global 
conflicts, volatile commodity cycles and shifting 
capital flows, yet your Company has stayed 
the course. We stand today at the threshold of 
what we believe will be the most consequential 
growth phase in our history, guided by a single, 
deliberate philosophy: “Strengthening Today, 
Cementing Tomorrow.” This theme is not a 
slogan, it is the operating discipline that has 
shaped every decision we have taken this year, 
from how we Leverage our land assets to how 
we choose the markets in which we build.
At Emami Realty, we see ourselves as more than a 
real estate company, we are creators of thoughtfully 
engineered spaces that seamlessly blend innovation, 
quality and execution excellence. Every project we 
undertake reflects our commitment to transforming 
ideas into enduring landmarks that enrich the lives 
Message from 
Managing Director and CEO
Every decision this year has 
been guided by disciplined 
execution from unlocking 
the value of our land bank 
to strategically selecting the 
markets that will shape our 
future.
Dear Shareholders,
of our customers and communities. Our approach 
is rooted in engineering discipline, where precision, 
planning and uncompromising quality guide every stage 
of development, from land acquisition and design to 
construction and final delivery. By embedding robust 
processes, stringent quality standards and meticulous 
attention to detail into every project, we ensure that 
each development upholds the highest standards of 
craftsmanship, reliability and customer confidence.
The Indian Economy: A Steady Anchor
India continues to stand out as the world’s fastest-growing 
economy, with real GDP reached at approximately 7.7% 
during FY26, its strongest pace since the post-pandemic 
rebound and comfortably ahead of every other G20 nation. 
Growth has been broad-based, powered by resilient 
private consumption, a sustained pick-up in government 
capital expenditure and robust momentum in financial 
and real estate services and construction, both of which 
expanded well ahead of headline GDP. 
Inflation has remained contained, external buffers are 
comfortable and policy continuity has kept the investment 
climate stable. For a capital-intensive, long-gestation 
business such as ours, this macro backdrop of durable 
growth, controlled inflation and expanding real estate 
demand provides exactly the kind of stable foundation 
on which multi-year development plans can be built with 
confidence.
Our Operations and Key Projects
Operationally, the year was focused on advancing the 
Company’s ongoing projects, strengthening execution 
across all developments and maintaining a disciplined 
approach towards project development and financial 
management. The Company continued to work 
15
Annual Report 2025-26
Financial Statements
Statutory Reports
Corporate Overview
towards timely progress of its projects while evaluating 
opportunities to enhance long-term value for its 
stakeholders. 
Emami Aamod, our flagship premium residential 
development and the benchmark for what we describe 
internally as the “Uber operation of Kolkata,” continues to 
anchor our premium portfolio, with strong construction 
progress and growing customer engagement through 
our site-level “Meet and Greet” initiatives. In line with this 
commitment to excellence, the project has achieved 
CII–Indian Green Building Council (IGBC) Green Homes 
Version 3.0 GOLD certification, this recognition is reflecting 
our focus on sustainable design, energy efficiency, 
environmental responsibility and creating healthier living 
spaces.
Beyond Aamod, we continue to advance a pipeline 
of projects across the premium residential categories, 
each built on the technology-led execution framework, 
aluminium formwork construction, the Falcon Bricks 
quality-monitoring system, Birla Pivot digital procurement 
and Microsoft Project-based scheduling that we believe 
differentiates Emami Realty from other developers in our 
markets.
Our Strategy
Our roadmap for 2026–2029 rests on two sequential, 
deliberately de-risked approach. We begin by centring 
on internal asset monetisation, extracting maximum 
value from our existing land banks to build a growth-
ready balance sheet by 2026–27. From this foundation, 
we will evaluate new land acquisitions and joint-venture 
opportunities, funded by the capital generated internally, 
with West Bengal as our primary theatre of expansion. This 
is a deliberate choice to play to our inherent domestic 
strength, our headquarters proximity, our decades-deep 
brand equity in Kolkata and our first-mover familiarity with 
a state now attracting unprecedented capital. 
Within our portfolio, we have consciously moved 
upmarket, targeting an 80% residential / 20% commercial 
mix weighted toward the upper-mid and premium/
luxury segments rather than the mass-market ₹50–80 
lakh category, in order to capture the rising purchasing 
power of the IT, GCC and corporate workforce now 
converging on Kolkata. This strategy is underpinned by our 
sustainability commitments, including the use of fly ash to 
reduce cement content and chemical curing methods to 
conserve water and by a culture that has earned us Great 
Place to Work certification, both of which we regard as 
essential to building a business that endures.
Real Estate: The India and West Bengal 
Opportunity
During the year, the real estate market witnessed 
a noticeable shift in customer expectations, with 
homebuyers increasingly seeking wellness-centric 
communities, environmentally responsible construction 
and smart, technology-driven living environments. The 
post-pandemic era has redefined the way people view 
residential and commercial spaces, presenting fresh 
opportunities for developers that can adapt to these 
changing preferences. 
Indian real estate remains one of the principal beneficiaries 
of the country’s growth story, with rising urbanisation, 
household formation and affordable-housing programmes 
providing durable structural demand, even as the sector 
itself grew strongly through FY26. Within this landscape, 
West Bengal represents a distinctly underappreciated 
opportunity. Despite being India’s sixth-largest state 
economy, West Bengal has historically lagged peer states 
in capital formation and industrialisation, with a real GSDP 
CAGR of roughly 4.7% between FY12 and FY25, the slowest 
among India’s largest states. 
However, the change in government regime in West 
Bengal marks an important inflection point for the state’s 
economic and investment landscape. The emergence of a 
dual-engine government, with greater alignment between 
the Centre and the State, it is expected to create a more 
conducive environment for policy execution, infrastructure 
development and investment. This alignment can 
potentially accelerate the implementation of large-scale 
projects, strengthen investor confidence and create new 
avenues for private-sector participation.
With approximately `1 lakh crore of investment already 
committed to the state under a changed policy regime, 
growing momentum in metro and railway infrastructure 
and the emergence of Kolkata as a destination for 
Global Capability Centres (GCCs), IT/ITES ecosystem and 
international corporate offices, West Bengal is entering 
what we believe is the early stage of a sustained, 
investment-led growth cycle. As one of the few developers 
head quartered in Kolkata, with an established brand and 
execution track record, Emami Realty is positioned to be a 
direct and early beneficiary of this transformation.
In Closing 
None of this would be possible without you. Our 
shareholders have stood by us through global conflict, 
market turmoil and cycles of uncertainty and your 
continued faith is the foundation on which we build. We 
are equally grateful to our employees, whose commitment 
to quality and service defines the Emami Realty 
experience; to our channel partners and customers, who 
we consider members of our extended family; and to our 
contractors, consultants and regulators, whose partnership 
makes every project possible. As we move deeper into 
this growth-ready year and toward the more ambitious 
expansion that lies beyond it, we do so with confidence in 
our strategy, discipline in our execution and deep gratitude 
for the trust you continue to place in us. We look forward 
to sharing this bright future with you.
With warm regards,
Dr. Nitesh Kumar
Managing Director and CEO

## Page 47

16
Annual Report 2025-26
Technology- 
Enabled Excellence
At Emami Realty, technology is becoming an integral part of how we plan, build, monitor and engage. 
Our focus is on adopting solutions that improve execution efficiency, strengthen quality, enhance 
transparency and create a more seamless customer experience. 
Building Faster, Building Better 
We continue to adopt advanced construction 
technologies, including aluminium formwork and the 
latest QuickDeck system from MFE. These solutions 
enable faster construction cycles, greater consistency 
and improved quality, particularly across projects with 
repetitive design elements. While they involve higher 
upfront investment, the benefits become increasingly 
evident through scale, repetition and enhanced 
productivity. 
Smarter Procurement 
Through Birla Pivot, we are digitising key procurement 
processes, including online purchasing and auctioning. 
The platform supports greater process efficiency, 
transparency and informed decision-making across 
procurement activities. 
Digitising Quality Assurance 
Our Falcon Bricks quality-monitoring platform brings 
greater discipline and transparency to construction. 
Built around a maker-checker framework, it connects 
contractors, site teams, engineers and head-office 
personnel, enabling real-time reporting, verification and 
monitoring of construction activities. 
Precision in Project Planning 
Microsoft Project (MSP) supports our project scheduling 
and monitoring framework. By tracking activities against 
established baselines at both site and head-office levels, 
the platform enables real-time visibility into project 
progress and delivery timelines across the project 
lifecycle. 
17
Annual Report 2025-26
Financial Statements
Statutory Reports
Corporate Overview
For us, technology is not an end in itself, it 
is an enabler of better execution, stronger 
quality and greater customer confidence. 
By combining construction expertise with 
digital intelligence, we are building an 
organisation that is more agile today, while 
creating the capabilities needed to shape the 
future of real estate.
Transforming Customer Engagement 
Our adoption of Salesforce is enabling a more structured 
and transparent sales ecosystem. The platform 
differentiates between direct and channel-partner 
customers, facilitates site-visit planning and improves 
lead tracking. Integrated customer interactions, including 
IVR-based enquiries and OTP-enabled site visits, help to 
create a more seamless customer journey. 
Preparing for an AI-Powered Future 
We are also embracing agentic AI to transform customer 
communication. AI-powered agents are being introduced 
to enable round-the-clock interactions, call recording and 
monitoring, with multilingual capabilities across English, 
Hindi and Bengali. This initiative aims to make customer 
engagement more responsive, accessible and consistent.

lakh sq. ft. of saleable area) and Emami Vana at Buda, Jhansi 
(approximately 45.5 lakh sq. ft. of saleable area), the last one 
being proposed for launch in phases. In addition to these 
projects, the Company is actively pursuing several other 
development opportunities that are at various stages of 
design and development and are planned for launch in the 
ensuing financial years.

14. Secretarial Audit Report
The Board of Directors of the Company based on the 
recommendation of the Audit Committee proposed 
appointment of M/s. MKB and Associates, Practicing 
Company Secretaries, having Firm Registration No. 
P2010WB042700 as Secretarial Auditors of the Company for 
the first term of five consecutive years from the conclusion 
of the 17th Annual General Meeting until the conclusion of 
the 22nd Annual General Meeting of the Company to be 
held in year 2030. The said appointment was approved by 
the shareholders at the Annual General Meeting held on 
September 24, 2025. 
Secretarial Audit Report provided by M/s. MKB and 
Associates is appended to this Report as Annexure 1. 
The Secretarial Auditor has observed (i) prior intimation 
to the Stock Exchanges under Regulation 29 of the SEBI 
Listing Regulations in respect of the proposal for voluntary 
delisting of the Company’s equity shares from The Calcutta 
Stock Exchange Limited was not furnished, and (ii) delay 
in disclosure under Regulation 30 of the SEBI Listing 
Regulations relating to re-appointment of the Managing 
Director & Chief Executive Officer.
The Board confirms that these were inadvertent, procedural 
lapses. As the delisting proposal was taken up as an additional 
item at the relevant Board meeting, the timeline for prior 
intimation could not be adhered to. The Board has directed 
the Management to ensure strict and timely compliance 
going forward. Applicable fines, if any, have been paid, 
and the requisite disclosures have since been made. The 
Company has also strengthened its internal compliance 
and review mechanisms to ensure strict adherence to the 
applicable statutory and regulatory requirements.
The Annual Secretarial Compliance Report as required 
under Regulation 24A of SEBI LODR Regulations has been 
submitted to the stock exchanges within 60 days of the end 
of the financial year.
The Company’s unlisted material subsidiary companies – 
Sneha Ashiana Private Limited and Delta PV Private Limited 
undergoes Secretarial Audit as per Section 204 of the Act 
and Regulation 24A of the SEBI Listing Regulations. A copy of 
their Secretarial Audit Reports is also annexed as Annexure 
1. The said reports do not contain any qualifications, 
reservations, adverse remarks or disclaimers.

28. Internal Financial Control System
The Company has an internal financial control system, 
commensurate with its size, scale and complexity of its 
operations. The Company’s system of internal control 
has been designed to provide a reasonable assurance 
with regard to maintenance of proper accounting 
controls, monitoring of operations, protecting assets from 
unauthorized use or losses, compliance with regulations 
and for ensuring reliability of financial reporting. The internal 
controls over financial reporting have been identified by 
the management and are checked for effectiveness across 
all locations and functions by the management and tested 
by the Auditors on sample basis. The controls are reviewed 
by the management periodically and deviations, if any, are 
reported to the Audit Committee periodically.

framework to identify, assess, monitor and mitigate various 
risks to key business objectives. Major risks identified by 
the businesses and functions are systematically addressed 
through mitigating actions on a continuing basis. It provides 
for identification of risk, its assessment and procedures to 
minimize risk and is being periodically reviewed to ensure 
that the executive management controls the risk as per 
decided policy.

Regarding 
Internal 
Complaints 
Committee:
Your Company is committed to providing a safe, secure 
and inclusive work place where every employee is treated 
with dignity, respect and equality. The Company has a 
zero-tolerance policy towards sexual harassment and is 
committed to ensuring a workplace free from any form of 
harassment or discrimination. 
In compliance with the provisions of the Sexual Harassment 
of Women at Workplace (Prevention, Prohibition and 
Redressal) Act, 2013 (“POSH Act”), the Company has in 
place a Policy on Prevention of Sexual Harassment and 
has constituted an Internal Complaints Committee (“ICC”) 
to address and redress complaints relating to sexual 
harassment at the workplace. The Policy provides a robust 
framework for prevention, prohibition and redressal of 
sexual harassment and enables employees to report 
grievances without fear of retaliation, ensuring prompt and 
fair resolution of complaints.

and NSE on 15th September, 2025, which was duly paid.

Regulation 30 of the SEBI (Listing Obligations and 
Disclosure Requirements) Regulations, 2015, in not 
disclosing in the outcome of the Board Meeting held on 
22nd May, 2025, the re-appointment of Dr. Nitesh Kumar 
Gupta as the Managing Director & CEO w.e.f. 30th June, 
2025, which disclosure was subsequently made on 14th 
July, 2025.

a) The Board of Directors of the Company is duly 
constituted with proper balance of Executive Directors, 
Non-Executive Directors and Independent Directors. 
During the year under review, there were no changes 
in composition of the Board of Directors under the 
provisions of the Act.

Board Meetings, agenda and detailed notes on agenda 
were sent at least seven days in advance, and a system 
exists for seeking and obtaining further information and 
clarifications on the agenda items before the meeting 
and for meaningful participation at the meeting.
c) None of the directors in any meeting dissented on any 
resolution and hence there was no instance of recording 
any dissenting member’s view in the minutes.
We further report that there are adequate systems and 
processes in the Company commensurate with the size 
and operations of the Company to monitor and ensure 
compliance with applicable laws, rules, regulations and 
guidelines.
We further state that recommendations have been made 
to the Company with respect to the compliances related 
to insider trading as required under The Securities and 
Exchange Board of India (Prohibition of Insider Trading) 
Regulations 2015.
We further report that during the year under review, the 
Board of Directors of the Company in their meeting held

Annexure 3 
Information Pursuant to Section 134(3)(m) of the Companies Act, 2013, read with Rule 8(3) of the Companies 
(Accounts) Rules, 2014 in respect of Conservation of Energy and Technology Absorption
A. 
Conservation of Energy
1. 
Steps Taken for Conservation of Energy:
 
Targeting Net Zero Emission by 2050, your Company 
started 
implementing 
various 
strategies 
and 
technologies to minimize energy consumption and 
maximise energy efficiency. 
 
Some key initiatives include:
 
a. 
Passive design strategies: Building Orientation, 
Daylighting, natural vegetation and insulation to 
reduce the need for heating, cooling and artificial 
lighting. 
 
b. 
Energy-Efficient HVAC Systems: High efficiency 
ventilation & air conditioning (VRF) system that 
significantly reduce energy consumption.
 
c. 
Energy efficient lighting: LED Lighting fixtures 
& sensors to reduce electricity usage for lighting 
while ensuring adequate illumination when 
needed.
 
d. 
Water conservation measures: Implementation 
of water efficient fixtures, rainwater harvesting 
and gray water recycling to reduce both water 
consumption and energy required for water 
heating.
 
e. 
Power back up system: installing DG sets of 
CPCB-II as per the emission norms to reduce NOX 
+HC emission by 62% and which in turn reduces 
the air pollution.
 
By combining these initiatives and incorporating 
advanced technologies like smart grid and energy 
storage systems, building can achieve net zero 
emissions. This approach not only reduces green-
house gas emissions but also creates healthier 
and more comfortable indoor environments while 
potentially lowering operating cost in the long run.
 
In our projects, we have added various facilities which 
will make the environment sustainable like Solar 
power generation, rain-water harvesting, pervious 
concrete pavement, reuse of waste water post tertiary 
treatment in STP, Composter and EV Car charging and 
washing point. 
2. 
Steps Taken by the Company for Utilising Alternate 
Sources of Energy
 
The Company is actively investing in green technologies 
to reduce emissions and minimize waste generation. 
Solar energy is the alternate source of energy 
integrated into our projects and their operations. As a 
part of the green building guidelines followed by us, 
solar energy is utilised to meet the energy demands of 
the common areas of our developments.
 
In our project Emami Aastha’s master plan concept, 
some innovative ideas in terms of facilities have been 
created like the creation of rain-forest by planting trees 
as per the guidelines of the Ministry of Forest and 
Environment that generates and boost oxygen. The 
project aspires to encourage biodiversity by changing 
environment and ecological potential vegetation 
strategy. This integrated green in the township 
provides micro-cooling subsequently reducing heat 
island effect and to create a natural habitat for native 
birds.

Corporate Overview
COMPANY’S PHILOSOPHY ON CODE OF CORPORATE 
GOVERNANCE
At Emami Realty Limited, corporate governance is an 
integral part of the Company’s value system and business 
philosophy. The Company is committed to upholding the 
highest standards of integrity, transparency, accountability 
and ethical business practices while ensuring compliance 
with all applicable laws and regulations.
The Company believes that good corporate governance is 
fundamental to sustainable growth and long-term value 
creation for all stakeholders. Its governance framework 
promotes responsible decision-making, effective oversight 
and equitable treatment of shareholders, while safeguarding 
the interests of employees, customers, business partners, 
lenders, regulators and the community.
The Company continuously strives to strengthen its 
governance framework through robust policies, sound 
internal controls and transparent disclosures, in line with 
evolving regulatory requirements and best governance 
practices.
BOARD OF DIRECTORS
The Board of Directors is responsible for providing strategic 
direction and overall oversight of the Company’s affairs. It 
oversees the Company’s governance framework, business 
strategy, risk management, financial reporting and internal 
control systems, while ensuring compliance with the 
applicable legal and regulatory framework. The Board 
remains committed to protecting the long-term interests of 
shareholders and other stakeholders through transparent, 
ethical and responsible decision-making.
The Board comprises Directors with diverse qualifications, 
professional expertise and experience across areas such 
as business management, finance, governance, legal and 
regulatory matters, strategy and the real estate sector. Their 
collective knowledge and independent judgement provide 
valuable guidance in achieving the Company’s strategic 
objectives and sustainable growth. The Board is supported 
by its Committees and the senior management team, which 
assist in the effective discharge of its responsibilities.
Composition 
The Company has an appropriate balance of skill, experience, 
expertise and diversity of perspectives on its Board, suited 
to the requirements of the business of the Company. The 
Composition of the Board of Directors as on March 31, 
2026 is in conformity with the provisions of Section 149 
of the Companies Act, 2013 and Regulation 17(1) of the 
Securities and Exchange Board of India (Listing Obligations 
and Disclosure Requirements) Regulations, 2015 (“Listing 
Regulations”).
As on March 31, 2026 the Board consists of 6 Directors which 
includes the Managing Director & CEO, 1 (one) Executive 
Director and 4 (four) Non-Executive Directors out of which 
3 (three) Directors are Independent Directors including one 
Woman Director, bringing in a wide range of expertise and 
experience to the Board functioning.

Board Meetings & Procedure
The Board functions through a structured governance 
framework that facilitates informed and effective decision-
making. The agenda for each meeting, together with 
detailed notes and relevant supporting information, is 
circulated to the Directors well in advance to enable 
meaningful deliberations. In addition to the matters 
specifically required under the Companies Act, 2013 and 
the SEBI (Listing Obligations and Disclosure Requirements) 
Regulations, 2015, the Board reviews the Company’s 
operational and financial performance, business strategy, 
compliance status, risk management framework, internal 
financial controls and other significant developments 
impacting the Company’s business.
The Board periodically reviews compliance with all 
applicable laws in accordance with Regulation 17(3) of 
the Listing Regulations and is kept informed of all material 
events and developments. Any item requiring urgent 
consideration may also be taken up with the permission 
of the Chairman and the consent of the majority of the 
Directors present.
The Company Secretary acts as the Secretary to the Board 
and its Committees and assists the Board in ensuring 
compliance with applicable statutory and regulatory 
requirements. The proceedings of the meetings are 
recorded and maintained in accordance with the provisions 
of the Companies Act, 2013 and Secretarial Standard on 
Meetings of the Board of Directors (SS-1). The minutes of 
the meetings of the Board Committees and the material 
subsidiary companies are placed before the Board for its 
information and noting.
Board Meetings held during the year
The Board meets at least once every quarter to review 
the Company’s operations and to consider, among other

businesses, the quarterly performance and financial results 
of the Company. 
5 (five) Board Meetings were held during the financial year 
on May 22, 2025, August 12, 2025, November 13, 2025, 
February 11, 2026 and February 23, 2026.

COMMITTEES OF THE BOARD
The Board Committees are set up under the formal approval 
of the Board to carry out the specific activities as specified 
in their terms of reference which exhibit the scope, 
composition, tenure, functioning and reporting parameters. 
The Board Committees play a crucial role in the governance 
structure of the Company and have been constituted to deal 
with specific areas/ activities which concern the Company 
and need a closer review. The minutes of the meetings of all 
the Committees are placed before the Board for noting. The 
constitution, terms of reference and the functioning of the 
existing committees of the Board is explained hereunder. 
Audit Committee
The Audit Committee assists the Board in fulfilling its 
responsibilities of monitoring financial reporting processes, 
reviewing the Company’s systems and processes for 
internal financial controls and governance, and reviews the 
Company’s statutory and internal audit processes. As on

Terms of Reference
The Audit Committee of the Company is entrusted with the 
responsibility to supervise the Company’s internal controls 
and financial reporting process and inter-alia performs the 
following functions:
i. 
Oversight of the Company’s financial reporting process 
and the disclosure of its financial information to ensure 
that the financial statement is correct, sufficient and 
credible;
ii. 
Recommendation for appointment, remuneration and 
terms of appointment of auditors of the Company;
iii. Approval of payment to statutory auditors for any other 
services rendered by the statutory auditors;
iv. Reviewing, with the management, the annual financial 
statements and auditor’s report thereon before 
submission to the board for approval, with particular 
reference to:
 
a. 
Matters required to be included in the Director’s 
Responsibility Statement to be included in the 
Board’s Report in terms of Clause (c) of sub-section 
(3) of Section 134 of the Companies Act, 2013
 
b. Changes, if any, in accounting policies and practices 
and reasons for the same
 
c. 
Major accounting entries involving estimates based 
on the exercise of judgment by management
 
d. Significant adjustments made in the financial 
statements arising out of audit findings
 
e. 
Compliance 
with 
listing 
and 
other 
legal 
requirements relating to financial statements
 
f. 
Disclosure of any related party transactions
 
g. Modified opinion(s) in the draft audit report 
v. 
Reviewing, with the management, the quarterly 
financial statements before submission to the board for 
approval;
vi. Reviewing, with the management, the statement of 
uses / application of funds raised through an issue 
(public issue, rights issue, preferential issue, etc.), the 
statement of funds utilized for purposes other than 
those stated in the offer document / prospectus / notice 
and the report submitted by the monitoring agency 
monitoring the utilisation of proceeds of a public or 
rights issue or preferential issue or qualified institutions 
placement, and making appropriate recommendations 
to the Board to take up steps in this matter; 
vii. Reviewing and monitoring the auditor’s independence 
and performance, and effectiveness of audit process;
viii. Reviewing the utilization of loans and/ or advances 
from/investment by the holding company in the 
subsidiary exceeding `100 crore or 10% of the asset 
size of the subsidiary, whichever is lower including 
existing loans / advances / investments existing as on 
the date of coming into force of this provision.
ix. Reviewing compliance with the provisions of the SEBI 
(Prohibition of Insider Trading) Regulations, 2015 at least 
once in a financial year and verifying that the systems 
for internal control are adequate and are operating 
effectively.
x. 
Approval 
or 
any 
subsequent 
modification 
of 
transactions of the company with related parties;
xi. Scrutiny of inter-corporate loans and investments;
xii. Valuation of undertakings or assets of the Company, 
wherever it is necessary;
xiii. Evaluation of internal financial controls and risk 
management systems;
xiv. Reviewing, with the management, performance of 
statutory and internal auditors, adequacy of the internal 
control systems;
